She penetrated to the mouth of the Ems, close to the German coast, and there sank a German torpedo-destroyer. E 9 sunk the German cruiser Ilela some weeks ago. Berlin admits the casualty, and says the lost vessel was S 126, one of a class ten years old, which when new had a speed of 30 knots. She carried 50 mett. •

===== 0002601_19141010_art0187.txt | included=true | included =====
HOME RULE. Mr. Oros&ld's Congratulations to Irish Party.

Mr. A. 11. Crosfield, on the passing of the Home Rule Bill, sent the following letter to Mr. Mahoney, president of the United Irish League in Warrington, and Mr. James Fay, the secretary : "I am writing to send to you, and, if I may suggest it, through your kindness, to other Irish friends in Warrington, deep and heartfelt con- gratulations on that memorable and historic event—the passing into law of the Home Rule Bill—which has recently brought to fruition what for so many of. .us has been the work of

. thirty years past. This event, with all its heal- ing influence, bringing as it does a joyful mes- sage of high hope and confidence in the future for Old Ireland, is no less certain to benefit Britain and strengthen the entire Empire. "Of that we have striking and inspiring ex- ample and proof at the present time, in which all of us, in whatever part of the United King- dom we live, can whole-heartedly rejoice, for it fortifies us all in the great work and the mighty struggle to which we have set our hands." , We are given to understand that the receipt of the' letter has given much satisfaction in the ranks of the Irish party.

===== 0002601_19141010_art0188.txt | included=true | included =====
ADULT SCHOOLS.

Electoral Anomalies and Prop:rtiona Representation.

THE DIGNITY OF ALL LABOUR.

Mr. Harry Sherburn, speaking on Sunday morning at the Sankey Bridges (Men's) Adult School on "Proportional Representation," gave many telling anomalies of our electoral system, and pointed out many striking weaknesses. Under present methods it was possible to have a Government in power in the House of Com- mons whilst it was only represented by a min- ority of the aggregate votes in the constitu- encies. In Wales, there was a strong minority of Unionist opinion, which had little or no representation in the House of Commons; like- wise in the home counties Liberal opinion had no representation. Under a system of propor- tional representation like that in vogue in Switzerland and Belgium, minorities would always be represented and be able to voice their opinions in Parliament.

By the aid of 9, diagram Mr. Sherburn de- scribed the voting system and method ot count- ing votes. The Bible lesson on "National Service in Industrial Life" was introduced by Mr. Henry Rowe. An instructive discussion afterwards took place on the application of idealism and economic laws to present conditions, the general trend of opinion being that the two must work together.

===== 0002601_19141010_art0189.txt | included=true | included =====
HARVEST-TIME REFLECTIONS.

The harves-i-thiLg-iving:s;:vic-e.s-W-e; held at the Bewsey Adult School on Sunday. In the morning and evening the speaker was Mr. T. Ellis Benson, of Manchester. In the morn- ing he spoke on Tennyson's poems, " The Lady of Shalott " and "In Memoriam." After dwel- ling on the themes embodied in the poems, he recited several important parts. The lecturette was thoroughly enjoyed. The Bible lesson dealt with National Service, "In Industry and Commerce." The first pdr- tion of the Bible lesson was taken from Zechariah. It was seen that, the prophet lived in days not unlike our own, when the founda- tions of law anti order were menaced, and most of the provinces of the Persian Empire were in a state of anarchy. Hardship and poverty had disheartened the people, and the continuous raids of hostile neighbours prevented the devel- opment of the scanty resources of their land. The Temple had not been rebuilt, religion had decayed, and the civic virtues no longer had sway. But the prophet almost succeeded in his efforts at restoration. He saw "that the first regard of a community, however straitened in means, be the provision of religion that truth and whole-hearted justice abound in the gates, with love and loyalty in every heart towards each other."

During the discussion it was pointed out that there is need to dwell on the essential dignity of all labour, no matter how humble its nature, on the importance of looking at employment as social service rendered to the community rather than a task enacted by an individual or corpor- ation; on the places for moral heroism in the market-place or workshop; and on the particular degree of responsibility for existing conditions incurred by every consumer. Much stress was laid on the economic effects of poverty, but equal emphasis needs to be laid on the moral deterioration inseparable from the sordid want in which thousands pass their days. Many economic re-adjustments were necessary before most men could feel they were giving of their best to society. In the afternoofi Mrs. George Dale delivered an interesting and instructive address to the Women's School. The choir rendered special selections in the afternoon and evening. Collections were given to the Belgian Belief Fund.

===== 0002601_19141010_art0190.txt | included=true | included =====
SINGULAR PARTINGTON FATALITY.

A singular accident which had a fatal result occurred at the works of the Partington Steel and Iron Company on September 27. Whilst on night duty Herbert Whitelegge (18), a skip filler, of Common-lane, Carrington, was engaged near a bin down which pieces of iron ore were emptied from a waggon, several of the pieces fell over the hoarding which protects the bin, and Wkitelegge being struck on the head by one of them was found in an unconscious condition. He was removed to the Royal Infirmary, Man- chester, but death took place on Friday. At the inquest the City Coroner, Mr. E. A. Gibson suggested that the hoarding around the bin should be made more effective. A repre- sentative of the firm replied that they had on order 150 new waggons with hopper bottoms, which would prevent a similar accident in future. . A verdict of " Accidental death" was re- turned.

PALACE AND HIPPODROME.

Large audiences are visiting the Hippodrome this week and enjoying the capital fare pro- vided. George D'Albert, who tops the bill, is a bright and breezy comedian, and pleases with all his numbers. His "Ragtime Wedding js very popular, and "Your dear old dad was " Irish" proves quite a "hit, so much so in fact that numerous copies of it have been sold in the building at threepence each, half of the amount realised being handed over to the Bel- gian Relief Fund. "The Little Colonel" is the title of a dramatic scena presented admir- ably by Ernest Montefiore and Co. Frank Hartley is an exceptionally clever juggler, who introduces many novel features and adds to the pleasure his turn affords by introducing an acceptable spice of humour. Victor and Louis cause roars of laughter as a golfer and his caddie, who experience much trouble in getting ' off the tee. Mertz and Whittle are a highly entertaining couple, who combine wholesome comedy with harmony. Borelli makes his ap- peal to the audience with patriotic songs, ren- dered in French and English. He also plays the piano in skilful fashion. Lily Eyton uses a sweet voice to advantage in well-chosen songs and a novel exhibition of shadowgraphy is given by Charles Torbay. Excellent war pictures are also shown.

Next Week's Programme.

An all-star programme has been arranged for next week, and it will include Capt. Fred Wood- ward's talking and juggling Sea Lions and Seals; Dacre and Lynton in "The Tenor and the Burglar"; May Stafford Smith and company in "The Sneak Thief"; Nervo, acrobat; Kitty Dale, vocalist; The nelsons, comedians; and Adkin, the motoring ventriloc List.

"The Cross in the Cacti" proved to be a magnificent two-part drama, and "The Gaumont Journal" depicted many startling war incidents. RUNCORN UNION. TENDERS FOR MINOR ALTERATIONS AND REPAIRS. The Guardians of the above-named Union invite TENDERS for certain Minor Alterations and Repairs at their Workhouse, situate at Dutton, near Preston Brook, according to Speci- fication prepared by Messrs. Wright and Hamlyn, Sankey-street Chambers, Warrington, from whom copies of sitch Specification may be obtained. Tenders must reach my Office not later than 12 o'clock noon on Monday, the 19th day of October. 1914, and must be endorsed "Tender for Building Alterations," &c. The Guardians do not bind themselves to accept the lowest or any tender. GEO. F. ASHTON, Clerk to the Guardians. Union Offices, Runcorn, Bth October. 1914.

THE ASSOCIATION GAME.

The home team at once got going on the right, but the visiting goalkeeper saved splendidly. After this the visitors' left wing broke away, but finished a good effort with a dead ball. The home team were now doing all the pressing, the visitors being hemmed in their own half, but could not score. Half-time: No score. Upon resuming Emmanuel made tracks for the home goalmouth, and after some exciting ' play the ball was forced dead. It was now Em- mantles turn to do the pressing, which they kept up. In the last quarter of an hour S. Roberts put the visitors ahead with a fast low drive, giving‘the goalkeeper no chance. This was all the scoring, and Emmanuel won by one goal to nil. PRESTON BROOK v. KINGSLEY , , INSTITUTE. These teams met on the ground of the former club. The visitors won the toss and decided to play against a very strong breeze. The opening exchanges were fairly even, but the home team did not combine as well as opportunities offered, and the game in mid-field eventually suffered by too much big kicking. Gradually the visitors got the measure of their opponents and worked their way towards the home goal, when John- son, with a good effort hit the cross-bar, the ball being sent wide of the mark from the rebound. This. brought temporary relief to the home de- fence, as soon after Johnson, the visitors' centre- forward, made amends by scoring the opening goal well out of the reach of the home custodian. At this juncture the home teagn rarely broke away owing to the visitors' good defensive play, and Johnson brought the home goalkeeper to his knees with a good effort, which was cleared at the expense of a corner. The flag-kick proved fruitless. The homesters now woke up, and transferred play to the visitors' end, and after lseveral attacks had been repelled, Jones equa- lised with a low shot. At half-time the score- sheet read: One goal each. The home team facing the wind, it was ex- pected the visitors would have the game very much their own way, but they made mistakes similar to those made in the first half, by big kicking, and the home backs defended their goal area very well. After twenty-five minutes' Play in the second moiety Johnson secured the lead for the Institute. Frog now the home- sters only made occasiopal runs to the visitors' end, and their citadel escaped downfall several times owing to the visitors' erratic shooting. There was not very much interesting play now shown, but Johnson managed to score again for the visitors, and at the call of time the result was: Kingsley Institute, 3 goals; Preston Brook, 1 goal. WARRINGTON AND DISTRICT LEAGUE. Minor Medal Competition. The usual monthly meeting Of the above League was held on Wednesday :night at the Lion Hotel, Mr. J. Wright presiding. The most important item on the agenda was the Minor Medal Competition, and it was decided to run the competition if sufficient entries were sent in. Healey, Ratcliffe, and &allow. SOLDIERS IN UNIFORM. Ought They to be Served in Public- Houses ? , During the hearing of a case at the local Police Court on Tuesday, in which Gunner John Dacey, of the R.F.A., stationed at Bulford, was charged with being drunk and disorderly in - Watkin-street, The Chairman (Councillor 11. Roberts) said , he thought it was understood that when in uni- , form a soldier would not be served. The Chief Constable: I have never heard any suggestion of that kind. , The Chairman: I understood that the Licensed Victuallers' Association had made 1 some suggestion to that effect? All the magis- trates received a paper. The Clerk (Mr. G. F. F. White) said there was a suggestion of that kind. The Chief Constable: Ido not think so. I have received a copy of a resolution they have passed, but I don't think it is so stringent as that. The Chairman: It seems to me to be particu- larly unfortunate if the members of the Trade concerned are not loyal to their advisers. The Chief Constable: It is palpable to anyone who walks 'round the town that soldiers are being served. Ido not know whether the' publi- cans' own committee have advised it. It is not a thing they could possibly expect would be .carried out. Continuing with the evidence, the Chief Con- stable said the prisoner had a pass and a rail- way ticket to London. He asked that the case should be dismissed, and that the police should see the prisoner in the train. The Bench agreed.

HARVEST FESTIVALS, ST. CLEMENT'S MISSION. On Thursday night harvest in-gathering cele- brations were commenced at the St. Clement's Mission. The decorations of the building were simple, the money thus saved being devoted to the Prince of Wales' National Relief Fund. ORFORD CHURCH. The harvest thanksgiving services were held on Sunday, the preacher in the morning being the Vicar (the Rev. G. Vale-Qwen), who took for his text, "Let both grow together until the harvest." The preacher at the children's service in the afternoon and at the evening service was the Rev. F. Owen, brother of the Vicar, who took for his text, The flowers appear on the earth." FORTHCOMING FESTIVALS. Harvest thanksgiving services will be held to-morrow at St. Paul's Church, when the preachers? will be the Revs. W. Braceaamp (vicar), H. F. S. Collier and E. Downham.— At the Winwick Wesleyan Church the Rev. A. J. Shields, of Didsbury College, will occupy the pulpit in the afternoon and evening.—The Rev. T. T. James, the Pastor of the Wycliffe Church, will preach at the harvest thanksgi:- ing services to be held at that church to. morrow. ST. PAUL'S. The first of a series of services of thanksgiv- ing for the harvest was held at ,St. Paul's on Thursday evening. There was a very good congregation, and the service was excellently rendered. The anthem "0 clap your hands" was nicely sung by the choir, the solo part being taken by Mr. Moore. A most appropriate sermon was preached by the Rev. and Hon. C. F. Cross, Vicar of Stretton, from the text: "They that sow in tears shall reap in joy." The services will be continued on Sunday. BRICK-STREET MISSION. HarVest thanksgiving services were held in connection with this Independent Methodist Mission on Sunday, Mr. A. Mounfleld being the preacher in the morning, and Councillor R. Henshall in the evening. The choir, under the leadership of Mr. A. Morris, rendered special anthems in the evening. On Monday evening a numerous company attended a fruit banquet, followed by a concert, the following contributing to the programme: the Misses E. Shaw and E. Lightfoot; Messrs. James Tom- kinson, R. PROGRESS OF THE WAR.

PUBLIC AMUSEMENTS. AN AUTUMN PANTOMIME AT TIIE ROYAL COURT. "The Pride of Byzantia's " Return. A pantomime at any other time but at the Christmas recognised pantomime season is in- deed a novelty, and Mr. Francis Laidler's new version of "The Pride of Byzantia," which pays a return visit to the Royal Court Theatre, War- rington, next week, can certainly be described by no other name than a pantomime. The attraction is entirely up-to-date, and although several of the principals were in the original production, the comedy business and the musi- cal numbers are quite new. There are no old pantomime songs in this new autumn panto- mime, permission for the very latest numbers, some of which have not yet been published, has been obtained, and the public are invited to come to the Royal Court next week to hear for the first time the songs which will be sung in ; an the principal pantomimes next season. ; The cast is composed of pantomime "stars," and Miss Mabel Medrow, who plays the title- role, is engaged as the Principal Boy in Mr. Francis Laidler's pantomime at the Theatre Royal, Leeds, next Christmas, to succeed Miss Clarice Mayne, who has played the Principal Boy in Mr. Francis Laidler's Leeds pantomimes for the last two years. Miss Mabel Medrow will, sing a French patriotic song, " Au revoir, my own sweet Marguerite," and one of the very latest publications, which is certain to be heard in all next season's pantomimes, "Kiss me 'Good-bye,' my little soldier boy," in which last number Miss Medrow will be supported by the "Eight Little Sunbeams," in striking uni- forms, as drummer boys. "The Eight Little Sunbeams," who caused quite a sensation on the first visit of "The Pride of Byzantia," will be again seen with the attraction next week in their latest numbers. Mr. Will Lindsay, who was the principal comedian in the Christmas production at the Grand Theatre, Hull, last season, and will be
